## 4. The first end-to-end test, written before the proxy

- [x] 4.1 Build the integration-tier streaming harness: one command that starts a proxy process, a sidecar process and a recording origin, waits only on their readiness surfaces, and tears all three down; verify by running it against stub binaries that exit non-zero and asserting the harness reports which process failed instead of hanging.
- [x] 4.2 Build the recording origin (Go) that records each request's method token, ordered (name,value) field pairs, body octets and a digest to disk and never decodes, validates or transcodes an octet; verify unit tests asserting that all 256 octet values plus a lone `0x80` yield a recorded digest equal to the input's, that three same-name header lines whose values cannot legitimately be combined are recorded as three ordered entries in the order received with received case preserved verbatim, that two lowercase and two mixed-case method tokens are recorded as four distinct byte sequences, that a raw target containing `%2F` and a trailing slash is recorded undecoded, and that a deliberately map-backed variant committed under `ci/broken-inputs/` fails the header and method assertions — the instrument is proven to discriminate before anything is measured with it.
- [ ] 4.2a Extend task 4.2's recording origin, building no second instrument, to read a chunked request body, recording the de-framed octets, their digest and that the body arrived chunked while still decoding no content octet, and still refusing without recording a request carrying both a Content-Length and a Transfer-Encoding field, as invalid framing, and a transfer coding other than a single chunked coding, as unimplemented; and add the two response modes task 13.3's suite-supplied origin backend does not provide: a declared representation length followed by a close after fewer octets, for task 37.10's truncation test, and a zero-length body stating its length on a status permitting one, for task 38.3's empty-body case; verify a unit test in 4.2's style that all 256 octet values plus a lone `0x80` sent chunked yield the digest of the de-framed octets with no chunk-framing octet entering it, a unit test asserting each of the two framings still refused is answered with its refusal and nothing recorded, replacing `TestATransferCodingIsStillRefusedAsUnimplemented`, a unit test per response mode asserting the status, fields and octets it emits, and that 4.2's map-backed broken variant still fails.
- [x] 4.3 THE FIRST TEST, WRITTEN BEFORE THE PROXY EXISTS — this task precedes every implementation task in this change and must be merged before any of them: write the end-to-end test that POSTs a body of arbitrary octets (every value 0x00–0xFF, an embedded lone `0x80`, total length a literal 1 MiB — chosen here rather than derived from the contract's floor frame payload, which task 9.1 does not define until five sections later, so this task carries no forward dependency and can be merged first as it claims) through the client edge and asserts the recording origin's digest and octet count equal the client's, making that assertion at the origin's recording alone so it is satisfiable before any response path exists; verify it currently fails with a diagnostic naming the absent proxy rather than an assertion satisfied by an empty body, and commit it under task 4.5's expected-outcome registry.
- [ ] 4.3a Make task 4.3's raw client return the origin's recording once the origin has written it, within a stated bound, rather than after the client connection ends, so the origin-side assertion is reached before any response path exists; give the raw client a response reader that delimits a response by the framing the edge states — a stated length, chunked coding, or, for a 1xx, 204 or 304 status or a response to HEAD, the end of the head, as RFC 9112 section 6.3 requires — rather than by the end of the connection; and register the client-side half of task 4.3, the POST's response read to completion by that reader and asserted to carry the recording origin's 204 status, as a gating test of its own in task 4.5's registry, recorded red with task 38.1, the first task to deliver a response head to the client, named as its closing task; verify a test driving the client against a stub listener that accepts the POST and never answers, with a recording placed in the origin's directory, asserting the recording is returned within the bound while the connection stays open, a test feeding the reader, each on a connection held open, a stated-length response, a chunked response and a 204 response stating neither, asserting each completes at its framing's end with no chunk-framing octet in the body it returns, and task 4.5's check passing with the new entry recorded red and naming task 38.1 while the new test fails with a stated diagnostic rather than hanging. This task is worked in the same change as task 36.5 rather than in file order: once task 26.4a's chain is wired and the request path is complete, the recording read is what turns task 4.3's gate green, so landing it at any earlier change would turn that gate green at a change other than task 36.5, which advances its recorded outcome.
- [x] 4.4 Write the response-direction twin of 4.3 in the same harness: the recording origin emits a body of arbitrary octets and the raw client asserts digest and length equality; verify it fails now naming the absent proxy and is committed under task 4.5's expected-outcome registry as a second recorded-red gate.
- [ ] 4.4a Make task 4.4's raw client read the response body through task 4.3a's response reader rather than to the end of the connection, digesting the de-framed body octets alone; verify a test against a stub edge that answers with a chunked body and holds the connection open asserting the client returns within its bound with the digest of the de-framed body rather than of the chunk-framed octets. It is worked in the same change as task 38.6 rather than in file order: once the response path is complete, the delimited read is what turns task 4.4's gate green, so landing it at any earlier change would turn that gate green at a change other than task 38.6, which advances its recorded outcome.

## 12. The adversarial fixture corpus, before any implementation it gates

- [ ] 12.1 Define the fixture format and its admission schema — every fixture names the requirement identifiers it exercises, whether it is edge-attributed, the role modes it applies to, its declared-capability preconditions, the bound any duration it asserts is expressed against (a contract bound of task 9.13 or one the subject is configured with), its supplied inputs and its expected observations — and stand up the minimal executable core the later fixtures of this group are verified against: a role-opposite counterpart peer, the suite-supplied client and origin backend, each also speaking the bidirectional-stream protocol, a specification-derived decoder, and a do-nothing stub subject holding the role under test, all completed and hardened by group 13; verify admission rejects a fixture naming no requirement, a fixture not marked edge-attributed whose named requirement does not exist in `contract/requirements.yml`, a fixture marked edge-attributed whose named requirement is not a client-edge requirement (`proxy/edge-hygiene`, `proxy/http-fidelity` or `proxy/websocket`) in task 7.4's registry, a fixture naming a contract bound task 9.13 does not define, and a fixture whose expected observations are empty, verify it admits an edge-attributed fixture naming a client-edge requirement, and verify one seed fixture executes end to end against the do-nothing stub subject and is reported unmet rather than unresolved.
- [ ] 12.2 Author the octet-fidelity fixtures: a lone `0x80`, a body containing every octet value, a multi-octet character sequence straddling a frame boundary, and frame boundaries at positions chosen to be hostile to reassembly — each present for a request body and for a response body; verify each asserts byte equality at the party beyond the subject in both directions.
- [ ] 12.3 Author the header-field fixtures: two same-name response fields whose values cannot legitimately be combined, repeated request fields, a name received in mixed case, a value containing a carriage return or line feed, and a header section beyond the declared bound; verify each names the ordered-header-pairs or section-bound requirement and that the bound case asserts a code distinct from the frame-size bound.
- [ ] 12.4 Author the framing-authority fixtures: a message arriving with both a content-length field and a chunked transfer-coding, and a backend response carrying a content-length plus a connection field naming a further field; verify the fixtures assert refusal rather than reconciliation and that no such field is carried over the tunnel in either direction — the demonstration against the reconciling counterpart is task 15.11's and against the baseline counterpart task 15.1's.
- [ ] 12.5 Author the request-target fixtures: `%2F` inside a path segment, a trailing-slash collection URI, a query with repeated keys and encoded characters, empty query versus absent query, asterisk form, an extended `CONNECT` upgrade protocol name carried beside an unchanged method, a control octet in a component, and a carried authority naming a host the subject is not configured for; verify each asserts the observation at the suite-supplied origin, and that the authority case asserts no connection is opened.

## 35. The proxy's dedicated proxied-traffic pipeline

- [ ] 35.1 Define one proxied-traffic pipeline at the adapter and endpoint level containing no body parser, no method override, no HEAD folding, no content negotiation, no session and no cross-site protection, and make it the only route by which a mount is reached; verify a structural test that enumerates the pipeline's stages for a mount-destined request and asserts none of those stages is present, plus a test asserting exactly one such pipeline definition exists in the codebase.
- [ ] 35.2 Prove the structural property behaviourally with the media types a parser would claim: verify tests sending a form-encoded, a structured-document and a multipart body and asserting the recording origin received each byte-for-byte with a non-empty length — the assertion is non-emptiness at the origin, not a 2xx at the client, because that substitution is the reference's worst defect. Tasks 36.2 and 37.4 are prerequisites here rather than passing citations: the recording origin receives a body only once the proxy streams it as body-data frames there and the sidecar dials the origin there, the origin reading the sidecar's own framing under task 4.2a, so this task is worked after both rather than in file order.

## 51. Reserved-path dispatch and route independence

- [ ] 51.1 Dispatch a request whose target falls under a reserved prefix at a segment boundary to the proxy's own handling before any mount lookup is performed, using task 20.9's enumeration and maintaining no second list; verify tests asserting a reserved target is handled by the proxy — answered for the tunnel-establishment and hostname-challenge paths, refused for the probe-surface and administrative prefixes under tasks 5.6 and 5.11 — even while a mount whose prefix would match exists, that no tunnel frame is generated and no sidecar observes it, and that a target merely sharing a leading portion with a reserved prefix is proxied as ordinary traffic.
- [ ] 51.2 Apply framing, syntax, size, and authority validation before reserved dispatch, so a rejected request stays rejected whether or not its target is reserved; verify a test sending conflicting framing to a reserved path and asserting 400 with the malformed-framing reason rather than the proxy's own handling of that target.
- [ ] 51.3 Bound the middleware exemption to mount-destined traffic so the proxy's own endpoints retain the ordinary request processing they require; verify a test asserting that a test-only proxy-own endpoint, built by this task under a test-only prefix it contributes to task 20.9's enumeration and declaring body parsing and cross-site protection, still parses its body and applies that protection while the mount pipeline does neither, and a check asserting that endpoint and its prefix are compiled only in the test environment and absent from the shipped build, so the proxy serves no unbuilt administrative surface. The same assertion against the real administrative or authentication endpoint belongs to the later control-plane change D17 defers.
- [ ] 51.4 Assert route-independence of the pipeline: send the same request to one mount by path prefix under a shared hostname and by that tenant's own hostname; verify the backend receives the same method, the same field sequence, the same remainder, and byte-identical body octets in both cases, and that neither route refuses a request the other forwards. Tasks 67.1 and 68.7 are prerequisites here rather than passing citations: a tenant's own hostname is a verified custom-domain claim, which carries no traffic until task 68.7 selects it by the authority established at connection setup, so this task is worked after task 68.7.

## 69. Protocol acceptance tests

- [ ] 69.1 Add the long-lived server-sent-events acceptance test on a route whose class has an unbounded total: a backend emitting an event every few seconds for longer than the ordinary class's total bound; verify each event is observable at the client before the next is produced, the exchange remains open past that bound, the client's byte stream matches the backend's by digest, and no media-type-specific branch is entered.
- [ ] 69.2 Add the large-range acceptance test: an open-ended range beginning partway into a resource several times larger than any hop's memory limit; verify the response is delivered incrementally, peak resident memory at every hop stays within its configured bound, the delivered bytes match the origin's by digest, and the exchange is governed by the idle bound rather than by any bound on octets held.
- [ ] 69.3 Add the extension-method acceptance test: a `PROPFIND` with `Depth: infinity` against a backend returning a large multi-status body, plus a `REPORT` and an extended `MKCOL` carrying a request body; verify each method token reaches the backend byte-identically, each response body reaches the client byte-identically, no hop aggregates a body, and no method allowlist is consulted.
- [ ] 69.4 Add the gRPC-Web acceptance test through the sidecar bridge for a unary and a server-streaming call, in the binary and the text-encoded variants; verify the success status arrives inside the in-body trailer frame, message frames are delivered as they arrive rather than after the body ends, structured error detail reaches the client with its octets intact, a status-only response still carries a trailer frame, and the text-encoded variant's memory held never exceeds the exchange's buffered-octet bound for a body many times larger than it.
- [ ] 69.5 Add the gRPC-Web refusal acceptance test against a mount whose only sidecar did not declare the translation capability; verify the client receives a refusal naming the missing capability, the backend never observes the request, and separately that a sidecar discovering the need after dispatch resets the stream with the capability named rather than delivering a partially translated response.
- [ ] 69.6 Add the WebDAV-style multi-status acceptance test across a mount: a `PROPFIND` whose body carries absolute `href` elements, a collection target ending in a trailing slash, a segment containing `%2F`, and a `LOCK`/`UNLOCK` pair; verify each `href` the client receives names a path inside the mount with no other element altered, the collection target is distinguishable from the non-collection target of the same name in the remainder, `%2F` reaches the backend undecoded, and both lock requests reach the same sidecar by the affinity key.
- [ ] 69.7 Add one acceptance-suite target that runs every test in this group against locally built proxy, sidecar and terminator binaries over both edge topologies and fails its caller on any non-conformant verdict; verify a deliberately regressed local build makes it fail naming the regressed test, and that an invocation which evaluated no test is recorded as a failure rather than a pass — the run against published artifacts is task 74.2's.
